# CodeView virtualized diff: migration ledger

Migrate pair-review's diff rendering from one `@pierre/diffs` `FileDiff` per file to a single virtualized `CodeView`, on `@pierre/diffs` 1.6.4, with no functional regression in PR mode or Local mode.

This file is the record of the migration: the decisions, the vendor facts the design rests on and where each is tested, the gate results, the hazards, the parity matrix, and what each phase did. It is updated at the end of every phase. The full implementation plan (architecture, per-phase steps) is the session document `PLAN.md`; everything a later reader needs to check the work is repeated here.

Branch: `fable-code-view`. The previous attempt is the local branch `pierre-code-view`; it is read for reference and never merged or cherry-picked.

## 2. Approach

Hybrid, weighted to a fresh port: re-implement the CodeView integration on today's main, keep main's contracts, and transplant a fixed list of functions and test assets from the old branch. The reasons, in short: a cherry-pick of the old branch conflicts in 8 files with 23 semantic hunks and merges real bugs silently; the old bridge renders zero annotations on 1.6.4 without logging anything (a vendor callback signature changed) while its 181 fake-based unit tests stay green; and main has since moved four contracts the old bridge was built against.

The rule that follows from the second reason governs all testing here: **the real vendor in a real browser is the only arbiter of what the vendor does.** There is no hand-written model of `CodeView` in this repository.

### Test layers

| Layer | What | Command | Arbiter for |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L0 | Console and page-error guard, stale-bundle test, entry-exports test, grep tests | part of `pnpm test` and `pnpm run test:e2e` | Boundary TypeErrors, a stale vendor bundle, forbidden patterns |
| L1 | Vendor contract suite: the real bundle, no app | `pnpm run test:contract` (`tests/contract/vendor`, `tests/contract/gates`) | Every vendor fact the bridge relies on; fails first on a bump |
| L2 | Bridge-in-browser: real bundle plus real bridge | `pnpm run test:contract` (`tests/contract/bridge`, from phase 4) | Bridge behaviour |
| L3 | App E2E, both modes | `pnpm run test:e2e` | User-visible behaviour |
| L4 | Golden render and landing dumps, FileDiff engine against CodeView | script, from phase 2 | Rendering parity |
| L5 | Live drive of the real server on a real repo | script, phase 9 | Real contents, untested surfaces |
| L6 | Performance comparison | script, phases 2 and 9 | No performance regression |

Rules for running them: never two Playwright E2E processes at once (they share ports 4000+workerIndex and one database); the contract suite binds no port and may run at any time; a failure set that changes between identical runs is flake, and the spec is re-run alone (`npx playwright test tests/e2e/<file> --workers=1`) before anything is concluded.

## 3. Decisions

Source tags in the copied tables below (`[P§n]`, `[P12]`, `[API§n]`, `[MAIN§x]`, `[DRIFT§n]`, `[OLD§x]`, `[TESTS§x]`, `[A§n]`, `[B§n]`, `[V]`) refer to the research reports and probe results of the planning session. They are kept so a claim can be traced; the in-repo evidence for a vendor claim is the contract suite (section 4).

| # | Decision | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D1 | No non-CodeView renderer for **diff** files. A missing vendor bundle shows an error panel in the diff pane. | The bundle is built by `prepare` and shipped under `public/` (`package.json:10-21, 36` [V]). The old branch kept the legacy fallback and paid with 19 double-pathed branches and no E2E coverage [OLD§d4, A§1.2]. CLAUDE.md asks for no legacy stubs. |
| D2 | **Decided by the user on 2026-11-14 (Q1 = B).** Context files are real `CodeView` items, interleaved by path among the changed files exactly as on main. One renderer for everything in the diff pane. A context item is a `type: 'diff'` item built from the file's contents (a zero-change, one-sided base) plus its ranges. | Position, order and every function main has on context lines are kept. The look changes to vendor highlighting, one line-number column and vendor separators (section 12). Verified on the real bundle before the decision (probes D2-1 to D2-6, section 12) and pinned in the contract suite since phase 4 (F20 to F25, G13). The legacy table renderer loses its last user and is deleted in phase 8. The plan's first default (a non-virtualized footer region after the diff list, drawn by main's table renderer) is gone from the plan. |
| D3 | The total highlight budget (900 KB / 18,000 lines) is removed. Per-file caps stay. | It protects a non-virtualized renderer. Under virtualization only mounted items highlight. Later files become highlighted instead of plain. |
| D4 | The "Large diff … Load diff" placeholder stays (parity). | A zero-hunk item plus a notice annotation; "Load" is an `updateItem` on the same id. |
| D5 | Content upgrade uses `hydratePartialDiff` for `change`/`rename-changed`. | Keeps git's hunks, 0 px shift; `parseDiffFromFile` shifts rows 60 px [P§6]. Main re-diffs today, so post-upgrade context rows next to hunks can differ from main by a line; classified as intended in the golden comparison (section 7.5). |
| D5a | **D5 is superseded (phase 7, the orchestrator's decision on H118).** The content upgrade of a changed file diffs its two contents again with the vendor's own `parseDiffFromFile(oldFile, newFile)` and keeps the lines of the patch on screen with main's patch-parity ranges: exactly what the per-file renderer does. | The user's requirement is no regression of the existing application, its look included. With `hydratePartialDiff` a reader saw other rows after a file's contents had loaded than on main (fewer context lines next to a hunk, a separator where main draws the last lines of a file, and in five files of the 60-file corpus other changed rows, H118). With the same function on the same inputs the rows are the same by construction: the post-upgrade stage of the golden dump is identical between the engines (section 11, phase 7). What D5 bought, that no row moves when the contents arrive, is kept another way: the viewer keeps the line at the top of the pane where it is (`bridge/contents.spec.js` "the line at the top of the pane stays there when the contents of its file, and of the file above it, arrive", 1 px). `hydratePartialDiff` is no longer called by the application; F15 stays pinned by its contract tests |
| D6 | `@pierre/diffs` pinned to exactly `1.6.4`. | The bridge reads three vendor internals (as main does), avoids one vendor bug, and since the phase-8 fix round replaces one method of a vendor class with a wrapper around it (F30, H162). The contract suite gates every bump. |
| D7 | Re-render (whitespace, refresh, scope) restores the first visible file's position. | Main tries to and fails (`window.scrollY` no-ops [MAIN§e5]). Exact restore is measured [P12]. |
| D8 | Line selection is viewer-wide (selecting in file B clears file A) [P§8]. | Main clears the selection after every use already [MAIN§a12]. |

### Questions that are the user's

- **Q1 (D2). Answered on 2026-11-14: B.** Context files keep their position (interleaved by path) and become `CodeView` items. Accepted by the user: the changed look (vendor highlighting and separators). Not accepted: any loss of function. Cost: about 4 more sessions.
- **Q2. Answered on 2026-11-14: "acceptable, but scope a follow-up; it can be filed as a GitHub issue".** Browser find-in-page (Cmd/Ctrl+F) will only see the 2–4 files mounted near the viewport [P§5]. On main a file stays in the DOM once it has rendered. This is inherent to `CodeView`; no vendor mitigation exists. The migration ships with this limit and builds nothing for it. Phase 9 drafts the text of the follow-up issue (an in-app search over diff data; with D2 = B such a search covers context files through the same records and the same reveal path as changed files) and the orchestrator files it on GitHub.
- **Q3 (permission, resolved in phase 0).** Phase 0 needs `pnpm exec playwright install chromium` for this worktree (about 300 MB). Another worktree's install deleted the build this worktree's Playwright 1.59.1 needs; only build 1254 is in `~/Library/Caches/ms-playwright` now [V]. The plan installs into a private `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH` so it cannot happen again.

- **Q4 (found in phase 1).** The E2E suite runs with CSS transitions and animations on. Main's fixture carried an init script meant to switch them off; it has never styled a document (hazard H41). The dead script was deleted, which changes nothing about how any test runs. Whether to make the original intent real (transitions off, a suite about 10% faster, further from production) is a suite-wide timing change and is the user's call. The measurements are in section 11, phase 1.

Q1 and Q2 are answered; Q3 is resolved; Q4 is open and work proceeds on its default (transitions left on). Phase 4 is the first phase the D2 decision changes.

## 4. Vendor facts and where each is pinned

Every fact was measured on the real 1.6.4 bundle in headless Chromium before the plan was written, and is now an assertion in `tests/contract/vendor/`. "Bug", "constraint" and "quirk" in a test title mean the test pins behaviour the bridge must work around; if such a test fails after a vendor bump, the behaviour may be fixed, and the fact is re-read before application code changes.

| # | Fact | Consequence for the design | Contract spec |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| F1 | `setItems` / `removeItem` that shift a mounted item's index leave an orphan host or mis-accounted height, and it does not heal. | The item list is replaced, never spliced. | `list.spec.js` |
| F2 | `setItems([])` + `setItems(next)` + `render(true)` in one task leaves 0 orphans and an exact layout, for the same list, one item removed above, one inserted above. A line scroll target issued right after it puts the first visible line back at the same pixel, in the same task. | This is the re-render primitive. Public API only. **The restore is a line target, not item top plus a pixel offset (section 6, item 9).** | `list.spec.js` |
| F3 | `render(true)` after `updateItem` is a synchronous flush: card connected, `onPostRender` fired, heights updated in the same task. | An annotation-only publish on a mounted item keeps main's "the card is there when the call returns". | `annotations.spec.js` |
| F4 | `render(true)` from inside `renderAnnotation` or `renderCustomHeader` logs `rendered a different diff than its prepared layout` and leaves a height mismatch that does not heal. From `onPostRender`, or deferred to a microtask, it is clean. | The bridge never flushes inside a vendor render callback. | `flush.spec.js` |
| F5 | With the worker pool, replacing an item's `fileDiff` does not change the DOM in that task; the old diff stays displayed until the worker returns (about 1 s for a 300,000-character file). Without the pool it is synchronous. | Reveal and upgrade are asynchronous in the DOM, as on main. Data-level answers must not depend on the DOM. | `swap.spec.js` |
| F6 | While such a swap is pending, an annotation-only publish plus `render(true)` does not create or attach the card; it appears with the swap. | No consumer may read "card not in DOM" as "line not rendered". Card lookups go through the bridge's element cache. | `swap.spec.js` |
| F7 | Derived metadata published with the same `cacheKey` is never displayed. `PierreContext.mergeContextRanges` copies the key. `hydratePartialDiff` assigns its own (`<key>:hydrated`). | Every derived metadata gets a new key (`_rekey`). | `swap.spec.js` |
| F8 | Without a `cacheKey` a remount shows plain text first; with `parsePatchFiles(patch, { cacheKeyPrefix })` it shows highlighted rows at once. | Keys are used, with F7's discipline. | `swap.spec.js` |
| F9 | A static `itemMetrics` gives an exact layout. Growth inside the header slot is never absorbed. The extent follows growth inside annotations within frames, also at maximum scroll. | Fixed-height header; no `setItemMetrics` loop; nothing that grows goes in the header. **Corrected by G9: two metrics are needed in this app, `diffHeaderHeight` and `lineHeight` (section 6).** | `layout.spec.js`, `gates/g09-*.spec.js` |
| F10 | A `lineNumber: 0` annotation is measured and tracks growth. An always-present empty one costs 0 px on ordinary and row-less items, given one CSS fix for row-less items. | The file-comment zone is an always-present first annotation. **The CSS fix is corrected (section 6).** | `layout.spec.js`, `gates/g11-*.spec.js` |
| F11 | Item and line scroll targets land with 0 px error, including never-mounted items. `scrollTo({ type: 'position' })` lands short (4960 for 5000). A line target set while that line's swap is pending is not retried. | No sticky compensation. Position writes use `root.scrollTop`. Reveal waits for the line to be displayed before the final scroll. | `scroll.spec.js` |
| F12 | After `updateItem` on the hovered item, `data-hovered` and the gutter utility disappear until the pointer moves; a synthetic `pointermove` restores both. With `pointerEventsOnScroll: true` hover goes stale after a scroll. | Port main's hover restore; hide the gutter on scroll. | `interaction.spec.js` |
| F13 | With the default `pointerEventsOnScroll: false`, item content takes no pointer input right after a scroll, including buttons inside annotations. | `pointerEventsOnScroll: true`. | `interaction.spec.js` |
| F14 | The `renderCodeViewFooter` region is rendered once, after the last item, inside the scroller; it survives `setItems([])`; sticky and `scrollIntoView` work inside it; clicks right after a scroll work. Growth above the viewport inside it moves visible content by the growth (`overflow-anchor: none` on the root). | Not used: D2 was decided the other way. The fact stays pinned by its contract test; no code depends on it. | `footer.spec.js` |
| F15 | `hydratePartialDiff` works for `change` and `rename-changed`; it throws for `new`, `deleted`, and zero-hunk `change` diffs; a pure rename stays header-only. | The upgrade strategy is per diff type. | `hydration.spec.js` |
| F16 | `renderAnnotation(annotation, fileOrDiff, context)`; `onPostRender(node, instance, phase, context)`; hosts are pooled and keep attributes; every update of an item replaces all its rows; per-instance `setOptions` / `setThemeType` throw. | Signatures from the contract suite; stamps reconciled on mount and update and stripped on unmount; row decorations re-applied from `onPostRender`. | `annotations.spec.js` |
| F17 | `expandHunk` on a partial diff throws without `loadDiffFiles`; `onHunkExpand` is not forwarded; the expansion map survives a `fileDiff` replacement keyed by hunk index. | Never call `expandHunk` on a partial diff; clear the map before publishing metadata with different hunks. | `hydration.spec.js` |
| F18 | The pool keeps highlighting off the main thread (268 animation frames with plain rows on screen against 1, for a 20,000-line added file); a worker script that fails to load falls back on the same instance. | Pool kept; main's fallback machinery is not ported. | `worker.spec.js` |
| F19 | A zero-change base from `parseDiffFromFile(file, file)` plus `mergeContextRanges` renders range chunks with expand controls. | The basis of D2 as decided. Refined by F20 to F25. | `hydration.spec.js` |
| F20 | The zero-change base is `type: 'change'`, 0 hunks, `isPartial: false`, with a `cacheKey` derived from the two files' keys. With ranges merged in, unified layout draws one `code[data-unified]` column: rows of type `context`, one line-number cell per row, a 32 px `line-info` separator per hidden gap labelled "N unmodified lines", a `lineNumber: 0` annotation above the first separator. Hover, the gutter utility (`getHoveredLine()` answers `{ lineNumber, side: 'additions' }`), `setSelectedLines`, line scroll targets, annotations and `collapsed` behave as on any diff item. | A context item needs no renderer, gutter, selection or annotation code of its own. | `context-items.spec.js` |
| F21 | In split layout the `change`-typed base is drawn in both columns with the same lines. With `type: 'new'` the vendor emits one `code[data-additions]` column and lays out additions-side annotations only; a deletions-side annotation is not drawn. In that column the vendor's CSS hides the separator controls and the app's `ANNOTATION_CSS` boxes a one-sided file into the right half; with `CONTEXT_CSS` the item is one full-width column with working controls, identical to unified. | Context metadata is one-sided: `{ ...base, type: 'new' }`. Annotations on a context item go to the additions side whatever their stored side. | `context-items.spec.js`, `gates/g13-*.spec.js` |
| F22 | Expansion on a non-partial item works without `loadDiffFiles`. A gap of at most `expansionLineCount` lines has one control that reveals it. A larger gap has a control per adjacent range that reveals the 20 lines next to it; Shift+click reveals the whole gap. For every gap larger than `expansionLineCount` the vendor also renders an "Expand all" control and hides it by CSS; shown, a plain click works. State is `{ fromStart, fromEnd }` per gap index on `instance.hunksRenderer.expandedHunks`, `Infinity` for a whole gap. | Main's one-click "reveal the whole gap" on a context file is kept by showing the vendor's own control on context hosts. A fold of the expansion map must clamp to the gap. | `context-items.spec.js` |
| F23 | `PierreContext.mergeContextRanges`, as on main, wrote `splitLineStart` and `unifiedLineStart` without the hunk's own `collapsedBefore`; the vendor's producers include it, and `VirtualizedFileDiff` lays rows out from these fields. With main's values, after the gap between two ranges is expanded on its upper side and then the gap before the first range, the item is one row taller than the vendor's layout and it does not heal. | `mergeContextRanges` is corrected (section 6, item 10). Not specific to context files: every `addContextRanges` on a changed file publishes such metadata. | `context-items.spec.js`; unit `pierre-context.test.js` |
| F24 | Replacing the list (clear-then-set, line-anchored restore) with a context item inserted above the reader: the first visible line is at the same pixel in the same task; a focused textarea inside a cached annotation element is re-attached with its value and caret and is focused again by `focus({ preventScroll: true })`; the line selection is restored by `setSelectedLines`; 0 orphan hosts. The vendor instances are new, so the manual expansion map is empty afterwards. An item whose metadata object and `cacheKey` are unchanged is drawn with tokens in the same task; a re-keyed one shows plain rows until the worker returns. | Adding and removing a context item at run time uses the list-replace primitive; the bridge folds manual expansions into ranges first, and re-keys only the records that had any. | `context-items.spec.js`; `bridge/reconcile.spec.js` |
| F25 | `parseDiffFromFile(file, file)` counts lines as an editor does: 3 for `"one\ntwo\nthree\n"`, where main's `content.split('\n')` gives 4 and main draws an empty line 4. Empty contents give 0 lines and 0 hunks without throwing. Cost measured for the decision: 4 ms for 3,000 lines, 25 ms for 20,000, 109 ms for 100,000. | No size cap is needed to build a context item. A file that ends in a newline has one line fewer than on main; hidden-line counts after the last range are smaller by one. | `context-items.spec.js` |
| F26 | **Vendor defect.** With the default item metrics (`hunkLineCount: 1`) an item draws a window of its rows and moves it a row at a time with the scroll position, updating the DOM in place (`FileDiff.applyPartialRender`). When the window shrinks at its end, `trimColumnRows` removes every element after the last row it keeps, including what belongs to that row: the separator after a file's last row (present once the file's contents are loaded and lines are hidden after its last hunk), the annotation row of a line, the no-newline row. Growth appends rows only, so the element is not drawn again when the window grows: it stays missing with its row on screen until that row itself is trimmed and appended again, or the item is drawn from scratch (remount). For the separator the item's layout still counts it (the validator reports the item and the pane's total on every pass, and the file below is drawn 40 px too high); for a card the row is measured again without it, so the layout agrees and the validator is silent. With a `hunkLineCount` larger than the number of rows an item shows, the item's window is every row at every scroll position and nothing is trimmed. `Infinity` and `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` were tried and not kept: a 20,100-row file drawn whole in the application was 39 px taller than its layout at rest (5,000 rows and 20,000 rows drawn whole in the contract harness are exact; not explained). | The bridge passes `itemMetrics.hunkLineCount: 5000`: an item that shows fewer rows is always drawn whole (as `FileDiff` draws a file on main); a longer one is windowed in 5,000-row steps, and the bridge draws it again when a window move has dropped a line's cards (H114, H119). | `row-window.spec.js`; `bridge/view.spec.js` |
| F27 | **The development validator reports a height the viewer has not followed yet.** When the element of an annotation of item A changes its height (a form opens or closes in the file's comment zone, a comment is saved) and a forced render that does not draw A runs before the next frame (a publish on another file, a bare `render(true)`), the validator logs `CodeView: sticky container height does not match computed layout` once: a forced render reconciles the heights of the items it updates only, and the viewer learns of A's change from its `ResizeObserver` on the list, which the browser calls in the next frame before that frame is painted. Measured: in the task of the render A's element is off the viewer's layout by exactly the change and the next item is drawn that far from where the viewer has it; a `ResizeObserver` created after the viewer's finds the layout exact when it is called; nothing is painted in between. A forced render that draws A (a publish on A) measures it in that render and nothing is reported. Seen once in phase 5 as `delta: -132.64` in a development-bundle run. | Not a defect a reader can see, and not avoidable from the bridge (every card can change its height, and only an update of its item makes a forced render measure it). The development-bundle E2E fixture holds exactly this report and makes the validator's comparison again in the frames that follow: equal, it is counted (`window.__layoutValidator.followed`) and the test goes on; still unequal after two frames in which the pane's box did not change, it fails the test as every other report does at once. (The same follows from anything else that changes a drawn file's height outside a render: lines that wrap again while a panel beside the diff animates its width, H139, pinned by a fourth test in `layout.spec.js`.) A persistent mismatch is still caught: by the item-height report of later passes and by the fixture's validating pass with the pane at rest after every test. | `layout.spec.js` (three tests named F27); `bridge/view.spec.js` (the zone, three tests); the fixture's rule in `tests/e2e/fixture-isolation.spec.js` |
| F28 | **The viewer's row renderer is told when the diff it is to draw next has been highlighted, and draws it in its next pass.** While a new diff of a mounted item awaits its highlight result the viewer goes on drawing the old one (F5), with whatever its expansion map holds at each draw. When the result arrives, `DiffHunksRenderer.applyHighlightResult` stages it (`pendingHighlightResult`) and calls the renderer's one callback, `onRenderUpdate` (the instance's `rerender`, which queues a render of the viewer); the next pass draws the new diff. Read in `renderers/DiffHunksRenderer.js` (three call sites, each after a result for the renderer's current diff was staged) and `components/VirtualizedFileDiff.js` (`rerender`), and measured frame by frame: rows 330 through every frame of the wait, the map emptied in the callback one frame before the new diff's 341 rows are drawn, no validator report. Drawn once with the old diff's map instead, the new diff showed all 400 lines of the file for one pass and the validator reported `sticky container height does not match computed layout ... delta: 1360` The renderer also says, before a draw, which diff that draw will be: `getDiffForNextRender(diff)` answers the diff it is handed when it can be drawn without waiting (a file drawn as plain text, a diff it holds a result for, a file whose present diff has not been highlighted yet) and the diff on screen otherwise; the viewer's own render asks it for the same purpose (`FileDiff.render`). Measured in the second session of phase 7: a reveal in a plain-text file after a hand expansion was drawn in the same task as the publish, with the old diff's map, and the validator reported `sticky container height ... delta: 80` (`evidence/phase-7/verify/probe-contract-nav-bridge.log`) | The bridge keeps the expansion map for the diff that is on screen and drops it in that callback; when the renderer answers that the next draw is the new diff, the map goes before the publish (H120, H146). Three vendor internals next to the map itself (`onRenderUpdate`, `pendingHighlightResult`, `getDiffForNextRender`); the fallback, one draw later, is in `_onPostRender`. The callback is not called for a file that is not mounted (the result is not staged while the item has no instance in the document: measured in phase 7's verification, 300 ms and 2,500 ms away, and pinned in phase 8), so a retired map is dropped when its file is unmounted (`_dropExpansionsOfUnmounted`, H146) | `bridge/contents.spec.js` "addContextRanges, isLineVisible and convertOldToNew on a mounted file" and "a reveal in a file drawn as plain text, after the reader expanded a gap ..."; `bridge/context-records.spec.js` "the reader expands a context file with real clicks ..." and "a stored range added to a context file / an uncoloured context file after the reader expanded a gap ..." (every frame of the wait has the expanded rows; validator 0); `vendor/internals.spec.js` "hunksRenderer.getDiffForNextRender names the diff the next draw will be, and onRenderUpdate is called when an awaited diff has its result staged, before it is drawn" (the three internals themselves, without the bridge); `bridge/contents.spec.js` "a file that leaves the mounted window while a reveal awaits display, after the reader expanded a gap ..." (phase 8); unit tests of `_retireExpansions` |
| F29 | **A file of `hunkLineCount` rows or more is drawn in blocks of that many rows, and the card of every annotation is in the document whether or not its row is.** Measured with the bridge's 5,000 (`ROWS_PER_ITEM_WINDOW`) on an added file of 20,000 lines: rows 1 to 5,000 with the reader at the top, 1 to 10,000 at line 6,000, 10,001 to 20,000 at the end; never more than two adjacent blocks (`evidence/phase-7/s2/probe-contract-window.log`). The viewer attaches the wrapper of every annotation of a mounted item to the item's element (`FileDiff.renderAnnotations`, one `div[slot]` per annotation) and draws the `<slot>` of a wrapper only in the annotation row under a drawn line. The card of a line outside the drawn blocks is therefore connected, has no assigned slot and no box (height 0), and `scrollIntoView` on it does nothing; the same holds for the line-0 annotation (the file's comment zone) when the first block is not drawn | "The card is in the document" is not "the card is on screen somewhere". The bridge asks the slot (`isElementDrawn`: the card's wrapper has an `assignedSlot`), in `scrollToAnnotation`, `whenAnnotationRendered` and `whenElementDrawn`, and `revealInDiff` asks the bridge (H154) | `bridge/annotations.spec.js` "a card on a row the viewer does not draw is in the document and not drawn; scrollToAnnotation and whenAnnotationRendered bring it on screen" |
| F30 | **Vendor defect.** With a `hunkLineCount` larger than the number of rows the pane shows, the rows of an item are drawn in blocks of that many: the block the middle of the pane's window is in, and the one before it (`VirtualizedFileDiff.computeRenderRangeFromWindow`: two blocks in all, starting at `round(centre block - 1)`). While the middle of the pane is in one block and its lower part is in the next, the rows of the next block are not drawn. The item's drawn rows are then held against the pane's bottom edge by the sticky container instead of being where the layout has them (the 5,000th row of a 5,300-row file, laid out three quarters down the pane, is drawn about 215 px lower; `heightAudit` `dTop` 206 to 221 px), until the middle crosses the boundary and both blocks are drawn at once. With other files after it in the pane the development validator reports `sticky container height does not match computed layout` by the height of what was left out; with the item alone it is silent. The last rows of an item that ends less than half a pane (plus the 200 px overscan) after a boundary, when that item ends the pane, are never drawn: not by scrolling to the end, not by `scrollTo` a line among them, not by a publish, a layout switch, or a collapse and expand (`evidence/phase-8/verify/probe-crossing-diagnosis*.log`, `evidence/phase-8/r2/probe-1.log`). With the default `hunkLineCount` of 1 the same formula covers the pane (the window is the pane's row count plus one, centred) | The bridge asks the viewer a second time. `PierreBridge.coverPaneWithRowWindow` replaces `VirtualizedFileDiff.prototype.computeRenderRangeFromWindow` with a function that calls the vendor's own, and, when its answer leaves rows out after the drawn ones that begin above the bottom of the pane's window, calls it again with a window of the same height whose middle is on the first row left out. The second answer is that row's block and the one before it, which holds everything the pane shows (a block of 5,000 rows is at least 87,000 px, the pane's window about 1,300). With a viewer whose first answer never falls short, nothing changes. The class is exported from the entry for this (H162) | `row-window.spec.js` (two tests on the viewer alone, which fail when the vendor covers the pane itself); `bridge/view.spec.js` ("a file of more than 5,000 rows is drawn where the pane is ...", "the last rows of a file that ends the pane ..."); `bridge/annotations.spec.js` ("navigating to a card on a line that was just revealed in a long file ..."); E2E `offscreen-mutations.spec.js` "The last rows of a file a few rows longer than one block of the viewer's rows ..." |
| F31 | **Vendor defect.** The viewer measures every row it has drawn with `getBoundingClientRect` (`VirtualizedFileDiff.reconcileHeights`; every row, because `overflow` is `wrap`), and the numbers of a `DOMRect` are 32-bit floats. From 262,144 px (2^18) from the viewport on, a float holds 32nds of a pixel, so a row whose height is not a multiple of 1/32 px is reported a 64th of a pixel off. The application's rows are 12 px at a line height of 1.45: 17.390625 px in the layout, reported as 17.375 or 17.40625 beyond that distance. The item's DOM is exact; the height the viewer computes from the measurements is not. A file of 20,100 rows drawn whole, the reader at its top, is computed 39.3 to 39.4 px short (5,038 rows measured off: 3,778 low, 1,260 high; `evidence/phase-8/r2/probe-whole-where.log`), the validator reports it on every pass, and every file after it is laid out that much too high. With rows of 20 px (the contract harness) every row measures exactly at any distance, which is why 20,000 rows drawn whole were exact there | The row window stays at 5,000 rows and is not raised to "every row": two blocks are 10,000 rows, at most 174,000 px above the pane. This is the cause phase 5 did not find for the 39 px of the 20,100-row file of `lazy-diff.spec.js` (H119) | `row-window.spec.js` "F31 (vendor defect): rows more than 2^18 px from the viewport are measured a 64th of a pixel off ..." (16,000 rows at the application's font size and line height: drawn whole the item is not the height the viewer computes; drawn 5,000 rows at a time it is) |

Also pinned, because the design leans on them: the annotation identity rules (which changes re-invoke `renderAnnotation`; a focused textarea keeps node, value and focus when another annotation is appended), a cached annotation element re-attached intact after eviction, an annotation on an unrendered line being attached with zero height and no error, `updateItem` with an unchanged version doing nothing, viewer-wide line selection (D8), `setOptions` as a replacement that keeps the first visible line and focus, `layout.gap`, collapse above the viewport moving nothing, the `collapsedContextThreshold` rule the visibility oracle copies (a gap of 5 lines or fewer is painted in full), and the shadow-DOM row model (`internals.spec.js`, `annotations.spec.js`, `options.spec.js`, `interaction.spec.js`).

## 5. Gate results

Gates G1–G6 of the candidate plans were run while the plan was written and are facts F14, F4, F7, F10, F12 and F15 above. G7–G12 were run in phase 1. Measurements are in `gates/*.json` of the phase-1 evidence directory and are re-recorded by `CONTRACT_EVIDENCE_DIR=<dir> pnpm run test:contract`.

| Gate | Question | Result | Consequence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| G7 | A worker that never responds: does `workerInitializationTimeout` fall back? | **Yes.** After the timeout the same `CodeView` renders and highlights on the main thread; `pool.getStats().workersFailed` is `true`. The vendor logs exactly one `console.error`, `WorkerPoolManager: worker initialization timed out after Nms`. Nothing is mounted while the pool is still initializing. | No bridge-side timeout. The value passed (15000 planned) is how long a user with a broken worker sees an empty diff pane; an E2E test that breaks the worker on purpose must expect that one error. |
| G8 | `pointerEventsOnScroll: true`: scroll frame times on the full workload | **No measurable cost.** 240 real wheel steps of 120 px, pointer parked on the code, shipped bundle, pool on: frame p50 / p90 / p99 / max = 10.5 / 16.7 / 27.2 / 27.3 ms with `false`, 10.3 / 16.7 / 27.2 / 27.2 ms with `true`; no frame over 33 ms in either. | `pointerEventsOnScroll: true`, as planned. No click-retry rule. |
| G9 | The app's real header (every variant), `ANNOTATION_CSS`, `pr.css` card and zone rules, the host without the 24 px margin and with `layout.gap: 24`: validator 0? | **Validator 0 and an exact audit in all four combinations (light, dark) x (unified, split), over 63 audited screens each including a walk with a tour active, once three things the plan did not have were added** (section 6, items 1 to 3). **Corrected in phase 5's fix round (section 6, item 17): the gate loaded `pr.css` alone, where the header is 45 px; the pages also load `styles.css`, whose `!important` rules make it 53 px in every variant (plain, renamed, generated, viewed, comment indicator, summary toggle, long path), collapsed or not.** The gate now loads every stylesheet the two pages load (`appStylesheets()`), runs on the CSS that is in `pr.css` and on `PierreStyles.unsafeCSS()` (no candidate text of its own), asserts the token (53 px) against every variant's natural height, the 8 px under a header, the file's frame and the pane's box, and is exact in the same four combinations (validator 0). | Required metrics, layout and CSS are listed in section 6, items 17 and 18. |
| G10 | Collapse on a swap-pending item, theme change during a pending swap, a second replacement before the first lands | **None of them interleaves.** Collapse takes effect in the same task (item height = header) and the layout stays exact; the new diff is displayed after the next expand. A theme change during a pending swap ends with the new diff displayed in the new theme. With two replacements issued in one task the intermediate one is never displayed: the observed order is `base` then the last published metadata. | The bridge does not need to serialise `fileDiff` replacements per item. |
| G11 | A `notice` annotation and a zone annotation both at `lineNumber: 0`: order and heights | **Array order is visual order; heights are exact** on row-less, ordinary and deleted items, unified and split. An empty zone is 0 px; filling it moves the notice down by exactly the content. In split style this needs the corrected row-less CSS (section 6, item 4). | The notice stays a separate annotation after the zone. No merge into the zone element. |
| G13 | (phase 4, decision D2) A context item built by the real bridge, with `CONTEXT_CSS`, the app's real context header (`PRManager.renderContextFile`), the app's real gutter buttons and the real split card layout pass, under unmodified `pr.css`: validator 0? | **Validator 0 and an exact audit in all four combinations (light, dark) x (unified, split)**, with wrapped lines, after a real click on a gutter button, a real click on every separator control (step after the last range, step before the first, both sides between two ranges, Shift+click, "Expand all" twice), a walk of the whole list, and a second walk after a reveal folded the manual expansions into ranges. The bridge read the header token and measured the row height itself (17.390625 px; equal to the rendered row). The context header built by main's code is as tall as a changed file's (measured as 45 px under `pr.css` alone in phase 4; 53 px with every stylesheet of the pages, which is what the gate loads since phase 5's fix round). A context item is one column as wide as its host in both layouts (`pr.css` gives a context file a 3 px left border and `styles.css` takes every wrapper's border away with `!important`, so the application shows none on either engine); cards, one stored with side `LEFT`, span from the gutter to the right edge. | `CONTEXT_CSS` and the one-sided metadata hold with the app's real code: no fallback, no question to the user. `gates/g13-context-item-css.spec.js`; measurements in `gates/G13-*.json` of the phase-4 evidence directory |
| G12 | Reveal latency on the current engine, 300,000-character file | **Baseline: median 1008 ms** from `PierreBridge.addContextRanges` to the revealed row being painted (5 reveals: 999, 966, 1093, 1026, 1008 ms), through main's bridge and `FileDiff` with the worker pool. The same reveal as a raw `CodeView` item update: median 1014 ms (1014, 1044, 995, 1051, 1001). Both were measured in the same run, four contract workers in parallel. | Phase 7's bar is this median plus 20%, re-measured in the same run as the new bridge. The reveal is asynchronous on main too: the data-level answer (`isLineVisible`) is immediate, the DOM lags by a worker round trip. *(Phase 7: measured in one test, one engine after the other, the new bridge as a third leg of the gate.)* Current engine median 808 ms (808, 791, 798, 832, 843), raw `CodeView` item 827 ms, `PierreCodeViewBridge` 860 ms (826, 827, 883, 865, 860): 6% over the current engine, inside the bar of 20%, which the gate now asserts (`gates/G12-reveal-latency.json` in the phase-7 evidence). |

## 6. Corrections to the plan found in phase 1

Each of these is proven by a contract test, changes a later phase's design, and supersedes the plan text it names.

1. **`itemMetrics.lineHeight` must be passed, and measured.** (Plan fact F9 and section 4.1: "row 20 px equals the vendor default".) `pr.css` sets `--diffs-font-size: 12px` and `--diffs-line-height: 1.45` on every `diffs-container`; custom properties cross the shadow boundary, so the app's rows are 17.390625 px, not 20. With the vendor's default estimate the validator reports mismatches and items drift from where the vendor thinks they are (`g09` "why lineHeight is passed"). The value has to be measured from a probe row, not multiplied out: 12 x 1.45 is 17.4, the browser lays the row out at 17.390625 (layout units are 1/64 px), and the validator compares with strict equality. The bridge passes `itemMetrics: { diffHeaderHeight: 45, lineHeight: <measured> }`. The measured value depends on zoom; see hazard H38.
2. **The scroll root may have no vertical padding or border.** (Plan section 3.2.) The vendor lays items out from the top of the root's content box and does not account for anything above it. Main's `.main-layout .diff-container { padding: 16px }` and `.diff-container { border: 1px }` put every item 17 px below where the vendor computes it. The vertical space moves into `layout: { paddingTop: 16, paddingBottom: 16, gap: 24 }`; horizontal padding on the root is harmless.
3. **Candidate CSS for the cut-over**, scoped by `.diff-view.codeview-engine` as phase 5 will scope it (source: `CANDIDATE_CSS` in `tests/contract/gates/g09-app-css-and-header.spec.js`):

   ```css
   .main-layout .diff-view.codeview-engine { overflow: hidden; }
   .diff-view.codeview-engine > #diff-container {
     flex: 1 1 0; min-height: 0; overflow-y: auto;
     padding-top: 0; padding-bottom: 0; border-top-width: 0; border-bottom-width: 0;
   }
   .diff-view.codeview-engine diffs-container.d2h-file-wrapper[data-file-name] { margin-bottom: 0; }
   .diff-view.codeview-engine .pr-file-header-slot { height: var(--pr-file-header-height); box-sizing: border-box; overflow: hidden; }
   .diff-view.codeview-engine .pr-file-header-slot > .d2h-file-header { position: static; height: 100%; box-sizing: border-box; }
   ```

   with `--pr-file-header-height: 45px`. No header variant overflows the fixed height. **Superseded by items 17 and 18 (phase 5's fix round): the token is 53 px, the scroll container has no border or padding at all, and the box a reader sees is drawn behind the list. The text above is what phase 1 measured under `pr.css` alone.**
4. **The row-less CSS fix is two rules, scoped by an annotation.** (Plan fact F10 and section 3.7: `[data-code]:not(:has([data-line])) { padding-bottom: 0 !important; }`.) That rule is wrong twice. In split style the 8 px is on `pre[data-diff]`, not on `[data-code]`, so the rule does nothing there. And it also matches an ordinary item that is mounted with no rows in its rendered window, where the vendor does count the 8 px, so it breaks those mounts. The correct `unsafeCSS` is `ROWLESS_FIX` in `tests/contract/harness/constants.js`:

   ```css
   [data-code]:has([data-line-annotation]):not(:has([data-line])) { padding-bottom: 0 !important; }
   pre[data-diff]:has([data-line-annotation]):not(:has([data-line])) { padding-bottom: 0 !important; }
   ```

   Phase 4 moves the constant into `pierre-styles.js`; the contract specs then import it from there.
5. **`instance.getLineIndex(line, side) != null` is not a "line is displayed" test.** (Plan section 3.10, `whenLineDisplayed`.) `getLineIndex` extrapolates an index for a line hidden in a collapsed gap, so it is never null there. The signal that works is `instance.getRenderedDiff() === <the metadata the bridge last published>`: while a swap is pending the item and `instance.fileDiff` already hold the new metadata and `getRenderedDiff()` still returns the old one. Once they match, the bridge's own metadata oracle describes the screen. `instance.isLineRenderable(line)` follows the rendered diff but answers `true` for a hidden line of a partial diff, so it cannot be used alone (`internals.spec.js`).
6. **F1 is timing-dependent for `removeItem`.** The height mis-accounting appeared when `removeItem` ran inside an animation-frame callback and not when the same call ran from a separate task, on this workload. The orphan host after an inserting `setItems` appeared either way. The design is unchanged (no splicing at all), and the finding says a "works when I tried it" result for `removeItem` proves nothing.
7. **The gutter utility needs its own stacking.** The vendor's slot wrapper is absolutely positioned inside the hovered gutter cell and, without a `z-index` on the slotted element, the cell's line-number content is on top of it and takes the click (which then starts a line selection). Main's `.pierre-gutter-buttons` rule already has `position: absolute; z-index: 20; pointer-events: auto`; it must survive the port.
8. **In unified style an all-added or all-deleted file's lone column is `code[data-unified]`.** `code[data-additions]` / `code[data-deletions]` exist only in split style. Anything that derives a side from the column element must take the diff style into account.

9. **Re-render restores the view with a line scroll target, not with `root.scrollTop = getTopForItem(id) + delta`.** (Plan fact F2, section 3.4, decision D7.) `setItems([])` drops every measured height. Right after the following `render(true)` the anchor item can be mounted only partially, with a wrapped line above the viewport still estimated at one row; when that line is measured a frame later the vendor keeps the visible line in place, so the pixel offset recorded before no longer means the same thing and the view ends up a wrapped line away (20 px in the contract case, deterministic). The planning probe saw 0 px because its anchor item happened to be fully measured. The primitive that is exact in every case tried (near the top of an item, deep inside a 3,000-line item; same list, one removed above, one inserted above): record the first row below the sticky header as `{ id, lineNumber, side, top }`, then after clear-then-set call `codeView.scrollTo({ type: 'line', id, lineNumber, side, align: 'start', offset: top - headerHeight })` and `render(true)`. When no row is visible (collapsed or row-less first item) an item target with an offset does the same job (F11).

10. **`PierreContext.mergeContextRanges` writes line starts the vendor's way** (fact F23; found by the probes for the D2 decision, corrected as the first step of phase 4). The plan listed `pierre-context.js` as unchanged. Main's function set each hunk's `splitLineStart` and `unifiedLineStart` to the running total *before* adding the hunk's own `collapsedBefore`; the vendor's producers add it (`utils/parsePatchFiles.js:296-297`, `utils/hydratePartialDiff.js:96-97`, `utils/updateDiffHunks.js:221-222` in the 1.6.4 dist), and `VirtualizedFileDiff` lays rows out from these fields. For ranges 100-120 and 300-310 main wrote `[collapsedBefore, unifiedLineStart]` = `[99, 0], [179, 120]`; the vendor's convention is `[99, 99], [179, 299]`. Under `FileDiff` nothing reads a layout from them, so main shows no symptom. Under `CodeView`, after a reader expands the gap between two ranges on its upper side and then the gap before the first range, the item is one row taller than the vendor's layout, the next item is one row off, and it does not heal. The module is shared by both bridges until phase 8 (H87); the proof that the correction changes nothing on `FileDiff` is in section 11, phase 4.
11. **No footer region.** (Plan sections 3.1, 3.12, 3.15 before the amendment.) The `renderCodeViewFooter` option, the `renderFooter` hook, `_mutateContextRegion` and the context-file region of `PRManager` are gone from the design: context files are items (D2). F14 stays pinned by `footer.spec.js` and nothing depends on it.
12. **A list write keeps the pane at its top when it was at its top** (found in phase 5; plan 3.4 and correction 9, which say the view is restored from the first row below the sticky header, always). On a page load the first list has no context items; the ones stored for the review arrive with the first `reconcile` (H92). With the line-anchored restore the reader, who had not scrolled, was left looking at the first changed file with a context file that sorts before it hidden above (measured on the real bundle with the rule switched off: after the `reconcile` that put a context file above the first of twelve changed files, the pane was at `scrollTop` 840 with that changed file still the first visible; `proof-attop-disabled.log` in the phase-5 evidence). On main the wrapper is inserted above while the pane is at offset 0 and is simply there, at the top: a browser does not anchor a scroll container that is at its start. The bridge now records `atTop` (`root.scrollTop <= 0`) in `_captureView` and `_restoreView` leaves the pane at 0 in that case; a scrolled pane is restored from its line as before. Pinned by `bridge/reconcile.spec.js` "a pane that is at its very top stays there ..." (both halves, validator 0) and two unit tests.
13. **Specs do change in phase 5, in more than the three places the amendment names** (plan phase 5, step 1: "Nothing else in the spec changes"; plan 7.3: "In phases 5–7 only the adapter changes"). Three facts the plan did not have:
    - *A virtualized viewer has no element for a file that is far from the viewport, and "far" is two files down in the four-file fixture pane* (measured: `zz/last.js`, the fourth entry of a pane of two small changed files and two context files, has no host). `expect(contextFile(page, f)).toBeVisible()` and `.toHaveCount(1)` state "the pane has this context file" in the table renderer's terms. N5 asked that 24 times; each is now `expectContextFile` / `expectNoContextFile` of `diff-dom.js`: on the default engine exactly one wrapper and visible (stricter than before for the `toHaveCount` sites), on `codeview` exactly one record of kind `context`, at most one host, visible where mounted. The sidebar-click scenario tolerates a null header position until the file is mounted.
    - *Decision D5 changes what a reader sees after the upgrade, by more than "a context line next to a hunk"*. The upgraded diff keeps git's hunks, so the lines after a file's last hunk are a hidden gap; main's re-diff shows three lines of context after the hunk and draws what is left when it is five lines or fewer. In the default fixture `src/utils.js` has 7 lines after its last hunk: main draws them, the viewer shows a second separator "7 unmodified lines" (golden: `separators` 1 against 2, post-upgrade rows 38 against 22 for the two files). N1 "hidden lines between two hunks are one separator" asks the named expectation `UPGRADED_DIFF_HUNKS` (`'rediff'` / `'patch'`).
    - *The scroll container moves* (plan 3.2). `slim-screen-panels.spec.js` set `scrollTop` on `.main-layout .diff-view` by name; it now scrolls through `scrollDiffTo` / `scrollState`.

    Every such edit is a named expectation or an engine-neutral helper in `diff-dom.js`, with the same or a stricter assertion on the default engine; nothing was loosened. The full list is in section 11, phase 5.
14. **One more branch site for the engine switch: `_removeRenderedFiles`** (plan section 6, the list of allowed sites). Local mode's empty-diff path empties the container itself (`local.js`, `manager._removeRenderedFiles(diffContainer)`); the container's children are the viewer's, so on `codeview` the same call gives the viewer an empty list (`_clearDiffItems`). `tests/unit/engine-switch-sites.test.js` lists the ten sites with their reasons and fails on an eleventh, and on a listed site that no longer reads the switch.
15. **`_fileStateKey` is not the only builder of the `context:` key** (plan 3.3: "nothing else builds the key"). `_scrubRemovedContextState` and `_noteContextItems` run after a context file's rows or record are gone, when `fileKind` no longer says `context`; they use `_contextStateKey(path)`, the function `_fileStateKey` itself delegates to. Every reader and writer of state for a file that is in the pane goes through `_fileStateKey`.
16. **The collapsed state is data on both engines** (H67 said the default engine could keep asking the wrapper). `PRManager._fileCollapsed` (state key to boolean) is seeded when a file is rendered (`renderFileDiff`, `renderContextFile`, `_buildDiffEntry`, `_buildPaneEntries`), written by `setFileCollapsed` (and, on the default engine, by the two legacy context toggles), read by `isFileCollapsed`, and cleared by `renderDiff` / `_removeRenderedFiles`. `isFileCollapsed` reads no element on either engine, and `setFileCollapsed` needs none. `setFileCollapsed` on a context path writes `context:<path>` into `collapsedFiles` on both engines (main wrote the bare path there when navigation expanded a context file).
17. **The file header is 53 px in the application, not 45 px** (gate G9's result in phase 1, and with it the token, the bridge's fallback and gate G13). `pr.html` and `local.html` load `styles.css` after `pr.css`; `styles.css` has `.d2h-file-header { padding: 12px 16px !important; border-bottom: 1px solid ... !important; border-radius: 6px 6px 0 0 !important; ... }`, which gives 12 + 28 (the file-comment button) + 12 + 1 = 53 px, and keeps the bottom border on a collapsed file (the rule of `pr.css` that removes it there loses to `!important`), so a collapsed file's header is 53 px too. Gates G9 and G13 loaded `pr.css` alone. Found by the verifier of phase 5 on the running application (53 px on the default engine, 45 px on `codeview`); corrected in the fix round: `--pr-file-header-height: 53px`, `FALLBACK_HEADER_HEIGHT = 53`, and both gates load the stylesheets the two pages list (`appStylesheets()` in `tests/contract/harness/constants.js`, which reads the pages and fails when they differ). The same stylesheet removes every wrapper's border (`.d2h-file-wrapper { border: none !important }`), so the blue left border `pr.css` gives a context file is not drawn in the application on either engine.
18. **Under `codeview` a changed file and the pane look as on main, to the pixel; the plan's layout section and correction 3 gave them another look** (plan 3.2, 3.6: "the vertical space moves into `layout`", a header slot of the token's height, nothing about a file's frame). Measured on the default engine with every stylesheet loaded (`tests/parity/visual.mjs`), and what `codeview` does for each:

    | What a reader sees on main | Where it comes from on main | On `codeview` | Height the viewer is told |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | A box around the files: 1 px border, 8 px radius, 16 px padding, a shadow; it scrolls with the files and reaches the bottom of a pane that is taller than its files | `.diff-container` (border, radius, shadow, `flex: 1 0 auto`), `.main-layout .diff-container` (padding) | `#diff-container::before`, in a one-cell grid with the viewer's list; the scroll container itself has no border, no vertical padding, no background | `layout.paddingTop: 17` (16 + 1) |
    | 24 px between files, and 41 px below the last one | `.d2h-file-wrapper { margin-bottom: 24px }` on every file, the last one included, then the padding and the border | no margin on a host | `layout.gap: 24`, `layout.paddingBottom: 41` |
    | Header 53 px, the page's fonts and colours | the header is an element of the page | the header slot is 53 px and sets what a slotted element would otherwise inherit from the viewer's shadow tree (font family, size, line height, colour, colour scheme: the vendor's `:host` sets all of them) | `itemMetrics.diffHeaderHeight: 53` |
    | The pane shows behind the header's rounded top corners | the wrapper has no background | the host and the viewer's sticky header box are transparent (`FRAME_CSS`; the vendor paints both with the code background) | none |
    | 8 px between the header (and the file's comments under it) and the first row | the top padding of the vendor's code block, which a `FileDiff` without a header of its own has | `.file-comments-zone::after`, 8 px high, on a host that carries `has-diff-lines` (stamped by the bridge when the item's metadata has a hunk) and is not a context file. The viewer removes the code block's top padding under a header. Its own option for it, `itemMetrics.paddingTop`, was read in the vendor source and not run (`utils/computeVirtualFileMetrics.js`: `getVirtualFileHeaderRegion` is the header's height plus that padding, and `VirtualizedFileDiff` starts every expanded item's layout from it): it is added to an item without a line too, which has no such space on main | measured by the viewer (the zone is the item's first annotation) |
    | A frame around a file's lines: 1 px left, right and below, 6 px bottom corners, rows clipped at the corners | `.pierre-diff-body` (an element of the page around the vendor's host) | `pre[data-diff]` in the host's shadow root (`FRAME_CSS`: border, radius, `overflow: clip`, `background-clip: padding-box` so that the translucent border is over the pane as on main) | `itemMetrics.paddingBottom: 9` (the vendor's 8 px and the 1 px bottom border; `PierreStyles.ITEM_PADDING_BOTTOM`) |
    | A file whose diff has no line (an empty added file, a mode change, a pure rename): a 1 px line under the header (54 px in all); a binary file: the header alone (53 px) | the empty body's bottom border; a binary file has no body | `.file-comments-zone::after`, 1 px high in the border colour, on a host that carries `has-diff-body` (from `PRManager._fileHostClasses`: the file has a patch, is not binary, is not held back for its size) and not `has-diff-lines`. The viewer counts no bottom padding for an item without lines, so the frame has no bottom border there, and such a file has no code background | measured by the viewer |
    | A file-level annotation's row (the comment zone, a notice) is on the code background | those are elements of the page above the code block | `FRAME_CSS` paints the row of a file-level annotation with the code background (the vendor paints it like a context line) | none |

    Every pair of numbers that must agree between `pr.css` and the bridge is asserted by `tests/unit/engine-switch-sites.test.js` ("the numbers pr.css draws the virtualized pane with ..."), the rules of `FRAME_CSS` by `tests/unit/pierre-shared-modules.test.js`, and the whole under the application's real stylesheets by gate G9 (validator 0, the 8 px, the frame, the box) and by the development-bundle E2E run.
19. **A context load that is overtaken does not settle before the load that overtook it** (plan 3.15.4 and hazard H80: "the later one wins, the earlier one stops at its next await", which said nothing of the caller that awaits the earlier one). `ensureContextFile` and `removeContextFile` read `contextFiles` and the pane right after `await loadContextFiles()`. With "stops and returns" the caller of an overtaken load went on while the later load was still in flight: `ensureContextFile` answered `{ type: 'context', contextFile: null }` with the file not in the pane, the tour skipped its stop ("Tour complete") and did not remove the context file it had created (the verifier of phase 5 reproduced it in both modes with a push arriving during the tour's own load). `_loadContextFilesCodeView` now: (a) an overtaken load awaits the newest load (`_contextLoadTail`) before it returns; (b) what the overtaken loads were asked for goes to the load that reaches the pane (`_contextLoadPending`): fresh contents, and "the reader is waiting", so that a push (`whenIdle`) that overtakes the reader's own load does not wait for a drag to end; (c) when the newest load ends without having applied anything (its request failed) and the diff is still the same, the overtaken load runs again; (d) a load overtaken by an emptied diff (`_clearDiffItems`) or a replaced one waits for nothing but that diff's own load. The default engine draws from every load by itself (and so can draw a range twice and leave one copy after its row is removed, a defect of main that is not copied; named expectation `CONTEXT_LOADS_IN_FLIGHT`).
20. **The content upgrade is a re-diff of the two contents, as on main; decision D5 is superseded** (plan D5, 3.14, fact F15's consequence; decided by the orchestrator in phase 7 on H118, recorded as D5a). `PierreCodeViewBridge.upgradeStrategy` answers `'rediff'` for a change and a rename with changes: `parseDiffFromFile(oldFile, newFile)` is the record's base, and the lines of the new file that the patch showed are kept on screen by `parityRanges` (main's `patchParityRanges`: one range per hunk of the patch that has a line of the new file), merged in by the same `mergeContextRanges` as a revealed range. Added and deleted files and files without a hunk are as before (`'none'`, `'reparse'`). A side of the contents that is missing, or two contents the vendor cannot diff (the same text), leave the file with the diff of its patch. The vendor's diff has **four** lines of context around a change (the default of the `diff` package), not three as H51 and correction 13 said. The named expectation `UPGRADED_DIFF_HUNKS` is gone: both engines draw one separator in N1's file.
21. **`revealInDiff` as built, where it differs from plan 3.11.** (a) *A line target does not open a collapsed file.* The plan's step 1 expands the file for every caller. A chat link to a line of a collapsed file goes to the file's header on main and leaves it collapsed; that is kept (`flashLines` skips step 1, the line is "not shown", `ChatPanel._scrollToLine` goes to the file). (b) *A line target is served on the virtualized viewer only.* On the default engine `revealInDiff({ flashLines })` answered `handled: false` and `ChatPanel._scrollToLine` took its own path, unchanged from main, so the two chat-link defects of H51 stayed characterized there. Phase 8 removed that engine, the `handled` field and the chat panel's own path: `_scrollToLine` is `revealInDiff` with a line target and nothing else. (c) *`scrollToContextFile` is not a caller.* A sidebar click on a context file must not open a collapsed one (N5, N4 for changed files); `_scrollToContextItem` asks the bridge for an item target or a line target directly, both of which reach a file that is not mounted. (d) *Step 3 is the bridge's `scrollToAnnotation`*, an instant line target (item target for a file-level card) sent only when the viewer does not draw the card (`isElementDrawn`: the card's slot, not `isConnected`; H154), and not when the file is on screen with a new diff awaiting display that will draw it (`_displayWillDraw`, H155), followed by the wait of step 4 (`whenAnnotationRendered`) and main's own `scrollIntoViewStable` for the landing; a card in the comment zone of a file whose zone is not drawn goes through `scrollToItem` and `whenElementDrawn`. (Corrected in phase 8: this item described the first session of phase 7, which asked `isConnected` and waited with a `whenFileMounted` that no longer exists.) (e) *The navigation that started last wins* on the virtualized viewer, whoever asked for it, and the pane is not moved for a target that is not mounted before every handler of the input event has started (H144).
22. **The focus gate is a typing gate** (plan 3.14: "while `document.activeElement` is inside that item's host"). A click on a line of code leaves the focus on the viewer's host; held back for any focus inside the host, a file's upgrade would wait until the reader clicked somewhere else, and its expand controls with it. The background upgrade waits while the focus is in a text field of the file (`PierreCodeViewBridge.isEditingInside`: `textarea`, `input`, `select`, editable content), without a time limit; it ends when the focus leaves the field, when the file leaves the mounted window, or with the render.
23. **The engine switch has one more branch site, `revealInDiff`** (the plan lists "`revealInDiff` step 3"; the read is at the top of the function, because a line target is decided there). `tests/unit/engine-switch-sites.test.js` lists it; its declaration pattern now also sees a method whose parameters are destructured over several lines.
24. **The plan's acceptance row "the navigation rows of N11 including a target outside the mounted line window of the large file" cannot be met with the corpus's large file.** That file has 4,000 lines and the bridge draws every item of fewer than 5,000 rows whole (`ROWS_PER_ITEM_WINDOW`, H114), so nothing in the corpora has a line window. The row is met with a file made for it: an added file of 12,000 lines in N11's "Navigation to a card far down a file longer than the rows the viewer draws" (more than 5,000 rows for a target the reader's block does not cover, more than 10,000 for a file whose first block is not drawn while the reader is near its end: F29), and a 20,000-line file in the bridge's contract suite. Found by the verifier of phase 7, whose probe on a 9,000-line file showed the navigation doing nothing (H154).
25. **The vendor entry exports four names, not the six of plan 3.18.** `CodeView`, `parsePatchFiles`, `parseDiffFromFile`, `WorkerPoolManager`: exactly what production reads, pinned by `tests/unit/pierre-diffs-entry-exports.test.js`. `hydratePartialDiff` left production with decision D5a (the upgrade is a re-diff), `getSingularPatch` was never read. The contract suite's `hydratePartialDiff` is H158. *(Fix round of phase 8: five names. `VirtualizedFileDiff` is exported for `PierreBridge.coverPaneWithRowWindow` (F30); the export test pins the five and still refuses a name production does not read.)*
26. **`patchParityRanges` is not deleted** (plan 3.18, "Upgrade"). It is a static of the bridge, part of decision D5a: the re-diff of the two contents keeps every line the patch showed. `tests/contract/bridge/contents.spec.js` compares against it by calling it (phase 8; it restated the rule before).
27. **`revealInDiff` keeps `findElement`.** The phase-7 text said the caller's own lookup goes with the old engine. Two callers still need it: an external thread that was filed in a file's comment zone or wrapper has no annotation (`AIPanel.scrollToExternalThread`), and the suggestion navigator hands over its own lookup (`SuggestionNavigator._findSuggestionCard`: the page's card resolver, then a document query by suggestion id, unchanged from main). What went is the lookup of line cards in the document (H156), the `handled` field and the `virtualized` branch.
28. **`CONTEXT_FILE_PLACEMENT` does not exist any more** (the plan's phase-8 acceptance says it "is `'interleaved'`"). With one engine it distinguished nothing and was deleted with the other named expectations whose two values had become one. N5 states the placement itself: `expectedPaneOrder` in `diff-dom.js` (each context file before the first file whose path sorts after it) is what its order assertions compare with.
29. **`engine-switch-sites.test.js` is replaced, not kept failing "when the switch exists at all".** `tests/unit/single-diff-viewer.test.js` is the grep gate of phase 8: the switch's names, the per-file renderer's names and the table renderer's names, over what ships and over the tests, helpers and scripts. The gates that already had a test of their own stay where they were (the list's one writer and no vendor `addItem`/`removeItem` in `pierre-bridge.test.js`; no markup written into `#diff-container` in `diff-status.test.js`; no document query for a comment zone in `file-comments-zone-registry.test.js`).
30. **Three signatures changed with the rows.** `showCommentForm(fileName, lineNumber, diffPosition, endLineNumber, side)` (no target row), `collapseSuggestionForAdoption(suggestionId)` and `_renderAdoptedUserComment(comment)` (no suggestion row). Callers are listed in the phase-8 log. `getFileAndLineInfo` answers from what a card stores and returns no `targetRow` / `suggestionRow`.
31. **`getBlockCoordinateBounds` went too** (plan 3.18 kept it "while the hunk-anchor and stats code calls them"). Its callers were the gap code and the legacy hunk anchors; hunk anchors are computed from the patch (`_computePierreHunkAnchors`). `hunk-parser.js` is `parseDiffIntoBlocks`, `countPatchStats` and `_stripTrailingEmpty`. `diffViewAvailable` (plan 3.19 says its gating goes) stays: the diff options hide the Unified/Split control when the viewer's bundle did not load, which is the state the error panel is shown in.


## 7. Architecture in brief

- The new bridge is `window.PierreCodeViewBridge` in `public/js/modules/pierre-codeview-bridge.js` until phase 8 renames it. Next to it: `pierre-annotations.js` (card builders, display order on a line, the split full-width pass, the element cache and renderer registry), `pierre-styles.js` (`ANNOTATION_CSS`, `ROWLESS_FIX`, `CONTEXT_CSS`), `pierre-gutter.js`, and `pierre-context.js` (range merging, the fold of an expansion map). The first two are shared with the current bridge, so the cards and the CSS cannot drift between engines (H6); every new file is wrapped in a function (H93).
- `PierreBridge` (new) owns the one `CodeView`, the worker pool, the item records of both kinds (changed file and context file), annotation arrays and the element cache, publish and flush, all vendor shadow-DOM knowledge, host stamping, scroll primitives, metadata derivation (hydration, context ranges, context bases, keys) and the two list writers. It never reads `PRManager` state; it receives hooks (`renderHeader`, `hostState`, `onItemLifecycle`) and, for the shared card renderers, the collaborators they need (`suggestionManager`, `PostedComment`, `escapeHtml`), never through `window.prManager`.
- `PRManager` owns domain state, the one ordered entry list of changed files and context files (`_buildPaneEntries`), header DOM for both kinds, file-comment zones, the content-fetch queue, navigation policy, the context rows and the cache of their file contents. It never touches `shadowRoot` or `window.PierreDiffs`.
- `#diff-container` is the CodeView root and the scroll container; the toolbar and tour bar are non-scrolling siblings above it; a sibling `#diff-status` shows loading, error and empty messages. Nothing writes `innerHTML` on the root.
- One `type: 'diff'` item per entry of the diff pane, id = the decoded path. An entry is a changed file (`kind: 'diff'`) or a context file (`kind: 'context'`); there is no `context:` id prefix, and `context:<path>` survives only as the persisted state key for collapse and Viewed. Ids are unique because of main's "diff wins" rule, applied where the list is built. Files without a renderable patch are still items (header-only), with a `notice` annotation where main shows a message. The list has two writers over one primitive: `renderAll(entries)` (a full re-render: every record is new) and `reconcile(entries)` (the set of context items changed at run time: every unchanged record is kept with its annotations, cached elements, metadata and revealed ranges). Both replace the list and restore the view with a line target (F2, correction 9, F24); the list is never spliced (F1).
- The file-comment zone is a `lineNumber: 0` annotation, first in every item's array (F10), on items of both kinds. A context item's metadata is `{ ...parseDiffFromFile(file, file), type: 'new' }` with its stored ranges and revealed ranges merged in by `PierreContext.mergeContextRanges` (F19 to F23); every annotation on it is published on the additions side and keeps its stored side in its data (F21).
- Annotation data is written first; cards are built once and synced on every `renderAnnotation` call; card lookups go through `getAnnotationElement`, which can build a card that is not mounted (F6) and never changes a card that exists (H71).
- Visibility is decided from the bridge's own metadata plus the instance's expansion map, never from the DOM (F5). Reveal is `ensureLinesVisible`, `scrollToLine`, wait until displayed (correction 5), then main's `scrollIntoViewStable`.
- During phases 5 to 7 the new engine sits behind `window.__PAIR_REVIEW_DIFF_ENGINE === 'codeview'`, set by the E2E fixture when `PR_ENGINE=codeview`, read once in the `PRManager` constructor. Phase 8 deletes the switch.

## 8. Hazards

**Existing code paths that do similar work**

- H1. Five "re-render then re-anchor" sequences: PR `handleWhitespaceToggle` → `_rerenderAllOverlays` (`pr.js:2843-2863`); `refreshPR`; Local whitespace (`local.js:530-547`); Local `_applyRefreshedDiff` (`local.js:943-947`); Local `_applyScopeResult` (`local.js:1691-1693`). A sixth, the Local base-branch change (`local.js:593-600`), re-renders without re-anchoring [MAIN§e5]. All reach `renderDiff`. Phase 3 routes the Local copies through the shared helper; the base-branch path is characterized, not changed.
- H2. "Render a file body, register hunk anchors, enqueue the upgrade" exists twice (`_renderPierreFileBodyNow`, `_renderDeferredDiff`); the deferred copy skips the upgrade. Both collapse into `_buildDiffEntry` plus the mount hook; keep the difference.
- H3. Two "reveal lines" implementations, `ensureLinesVisible` and `expandForSuggestion`, with different padding and collapse semantics [MAIN§e8]. With D2 = B `expandForSuggestion` has no file left to serve and is deleted in phase 8; until then it serves context files on the default engine only. `ensureLinesVisible`'s padding (`contextPadding`) is what a revealed line of a context file gets on the `codeview` engine.
- H4. Comment-card markup exists in the bridge renderer and in `CommentManager.displayUserComment`; header-button markup exists three times [MAIN§e8]. Posted-comment stamping must be applied in every copy that stays routed. After phase 8 one card renderer is left (the annotation renderer, in `pierre-annotations.js` since phase 4) and two header builders (`_buildFileHeader`, `_buildContextFileHeader`); the button markup they share is one helper.
- H5. `local.js` patches `PRManager` methods. Any method renamed or split in `pr.js` must be checked against the patches in `local.js`.
- H6. During phases 5–7 two bridges exist. Shared renderers live in `pierre-annotations.js` so they cannot drift; the switch-site test bounds the branching.

**Shared functions that change, and all their callers**

- H7. `renderDiff`: `loadAndDisplayFiles` (PR initial, whitespace, `refreshPR`), `loadLocalDiff` (Local initial, whitespace, refresh, scope, base). Every caller gets clear-then-set and anchor restore; tour exit stays before teardown.
- H8. `ensureFileBodyRendered` (deleted): `toggleFileCollapse:5154`, `toggleFileViewed:5201`, `expandForSuggestion:5828`, `ensureLinesVisible:5968`, `scrollToFile:8114`, `scroll-into-view.js:125`, `AIPanel.scrollToFinding:1426`, `scrollToComment:1529`, `scrollToExternalThread:1626`, `SuggestionNavigator.ensureSuggestionVisible:285`, `SuggestionManager.displayAISuggestions:334`. Each call site is replaced, not just removed: several used its return value as "the body element".
- H9. `ensureLinesVisible`: `review:expand_hunk` handler (`pr.js:2679`), `_fetchUserCommentsForRender:6689`, `_renderAdoptedUserComment:6900`, `SuggestionManager.displayAISuggestions:352`, `ExternalCommentManager:457` and `:624`, `AIPanel.scrollToFinding:1433`, `scrollToComment:1536`, `ChatPanel._scrollToLine:5960/5983`, `TourRenderer.prepareStop:251`. Contract kept: `isLineVisible` is true when it resolves. New: the DOM may lag by a worker round-trip (F5), and files may be unmounted.
- H10. `toggleFileCollapse`: header chevron and click, `toggleGeneratedFile`, `AIPanel.expandFileIfCollapsed`, `SuggestionNavigator.ensureSuggestionVisible`, `TourRenderer._mountStopPierre` and its unmount restore. The header callers have a mounted file; the others may not.
- H11. `loadUserComments`: init in both modes, `_rerenderAllOverlays`, Local whitespace/refresh/scope, `_handleCommentFormSubmit`, `restoreUserComment`, `clearAllUserComments`, `loadContextFiles`, the deferred re-anchor, the `commentFilterChanged` listener. Its clear-and-add block must stay synchronous inside one `bridge.batch`; the generation and version retry guard (`pr.js:6548-6576`) must be preserved; no `await` may be added inside the block.
- H12. `pierreBridge.files.has/get` (`pr.js:1531, 1862, 3788, 4252, 5168, 5192, 5209, 5859, 5971, 6063, 6592, 6897, 7046`; `suggestion-manager.js:380, 473`; `external-comment-manager.js:531`; `tour-renderer.js:566`; `ChatPanel.js:5955, 5986`; `comment-manager.js:321` [MAIN§b]): meaning changes from "rendered" to "has an item", for changed files and context files (H83). `files.get(f).container` (2 sites) and `.instance` (E2E only) go away.
- H13. `findFileElement`: returns `null` for an unmounted file (phase 3 audit).
- H14. `bridge.addAnnotation` (`pr.js:6065, 6906, 7073`; `hunk-summary-renderer.js:179`; `external-comment-manager.js:637`; `tour-renderer.js:421`) and `addAnnotations` (`pr.js:6615`; `suggestion-manager.js:402`); `removeAnnotation`, `removeAnnotationsByType`, `getAnnotations` [MAIN§b]. `applyServerComment` (`pr.js:7537`) mutates `metadata.data` in place and relies on the stored reference; that stays valid and `sync` picks it up.
- H15. `FileCommentManager.loadFileComments(comments, suggestions)` (null leaves alone, array replaces): `_loadUserComments`, `_removeRenderedSuggestions`, suggestion display. All must reach unmounted zones through the registry.
- H16. `_materializeDeferredDiff`: the Load button, `expandForSuggestion`, `ensureLinesVisible`.
- H17. `ScrollUtils.scrollIntoViewStable`: file scroll, the three AIPanel paths, the navigator, context-file scroll (`scrollToContextFile`, which hands it a chunk `<tbody>` today and goes through `revealInDiff` on the `codeview` engine). It must never be handed a wrapper for an unmounted file.
- H18. `_renderAnnotation` and the element cache: the vendor callback, `getAnnotationElement`, every `find*Card`. One code path builds; `sync` runs when a card is built and on every draw by the vendor (the `renderAnnotation` callback), and not when a lookup hands back a card that exists (H71, corrected in phase 3).

**State that can change between scheduling and execution**

- H19. Virtualization unmount between any `await` and the DOM use after it. Rule: after an `await`, re-resolve through `getAnnotationElement` or `findFileElement`; never hold a host or row across an `await`.
- H20. `renderAll` during an in-flight content fetch, hunk-summary fetch, suggestion display or navigation. `_renderGen`, the abort controller, `_displayEpoch`, `_navGen`, `_tourGen` guard these; `_publish` adds the re-`getItem` check; the `when*` waits take a cancel predicate.
- H21. A diff swap pending on an item (F5): annotation publishes are held (F6); a scroll target set now lands on a separator (F11); a second replacement can be issued before the first lands (G10); the user can collapse the file meanwhile.
- H22. A background upgrade landing while the user types in that file, hovers it, or is mid-smooth-scroll to a line in it. Pointer gate, new focus gate (3.14).
- H23. The pool not ready during the first 40 ms or so: nothing is mounted and a flush does nothing [P12]. No consumer may read "card not in DOM" as "line not renderable".
- H24. Filter flips, publication refreshes and suggestion status changes while a card is detached or not yet built. Covered by data-first writes plus `sync`.
- H25. A pending vendor scroll target is cancelled by any `keydown`, `wheel`, `pointerdown` or `touchstart` on the root, including keys typed in a comment form [API§3.15]. Navigation must tolerate "never arrived".
- H26. Width changes when panels dock or float (#597) change wrapped-line heights. The vendor observes the root [API§3.16]; `slim-screen-panels` and `panel-resize` run under the switch in phase 5.
- H27. A context item added or removed above the line in view. It is a list replace (the `reconcile` writer), not a DOM insertion: the view is restored from a line target, and everything the vendor instances held is restored or folded (H81). N5 "does not move it" and the L2 `reconcile` tests.
- H28. A reused `cacheKey` on derived metadata (F7): silent non-render. One `_rekey` function, a unit test, an L2 test.

**Completion and error handlers that assume they are alone**
